- An NPS page can be wrong about a building's material (the Lockkeeper's House is called brick; the photograph shows stone): look at a picture before taking brick from a text.
- `page.py` read `blm.gov` pages in full; the BLM's `blm.gov/visit/<site>` pages give the site's coordinate (Panaca kilns, within 13 m of BLM photographs on Commons).
- The Forest Service's new recreation pages (`fs.usda.gov/r08/<forest>/recreation/<name>`) read in full with `page.py --all`: "Operational Hours", fees, directions. Their "Hours: Monday - Friday 8:00 AM - 4:30 PM" line is the ranger district office, not the site.
- **When `page.py` says DRAWN BY SCRIPT, or prints only a menu or one flat line, read on:** the few characters it got often hold the hours (the Kodiak museum, Warren's library, the Garrett County museums, St. Peter Cathedral in Marquette, a Wix site in Jefferson County, New York), and the saved copy in `research/pages/`, or a plain `requests` fetch, often holds what the reader dropped: strip the tags (Squarespace and GoDaddy sites such as Shadows-on-the-Teche and Kent House, the Earhart museum, the John Brown Museum, the Keweenaw County Historical Society, cchsny.org). A Squarespace home page's source can carry the hours in its `openingHours` field, and a library's in a JSON blob (`"days":{"Monday":[{"From":...`), which `hours.py page` prints as one long line. `research/pages/` names its files by hash: find a page by its first line (`head -c 150`). Where all of that fails, a ten-line Playwright script read script-drawn pages in full (Minnesota: MNHS and DNR pages, whose hours rows `page.py` and `hours.py` read empty): `chromium.launch` with the `HTTPS_PROXY` address as its proxy, `page.goto(url, waitUntil='networkidle')`, then `innerText('body')`. Some sites time out on networkidle but still return their text; a Cloudflare check stops it.
- `page.py --all` prints one long line: `tr -s ' .'` then `grep -oiE '.{0,110}(a\.m|p\.m).{0,140}'` or `grep -o 'Hours.\{0,200\}'` pulls the hours out.
- `hours.py page` prints only lines with clock times: it drops season lines ("July to August") and rows with no time ("closed for the season"), and can show a time without what it is for; read the same page with `page.py --all` and search. It missed a table (the Springfield library) and a notice (airmuseum.ca) that `page.py --all` and grep found; it sometimes prints a day row that `page.py` drops (City Winery): trust what was printed. It failed on a TLS chain (huntersynagogue.org) and a reset (unyumc.org) where the web fetch tool read both.
- Hotel and restaurant sites keep their hours on a page of their own (`/restaurant/`, `hours.htm`, or a `getbento.com/location/...` page): grep the home page's links for "hour", "dine", "restaurant".
- Guessed sub-pages can all return the home page (the Cutchogue-New Suffolk Historical Council): grep the cached home page in `research/pages/` for the real `href` addresses. A WordPress page drawn by script often reads in full at its `?p=<n>` address (Old Westbury Gardens `?p=4879`, Sands Point `?p=6519`).
- Many small historical societies' sites do not connect at all: do not spend more than two tries on each. Check a business's news before taking its own hours page: Pleasant Valley's winery page still gave hours after a July 2026 state release said it shut in 2025.
- **Worship times from the denomination's own list,** where a search gives only directories: the Episcopal Asset Map (`episcopalassetmap.org/node/<n>` and `/dioceses/<diocese>/list/<church>`; search with allowed_domains set to it) reads cleanly, but a node found by search is often another church of the same name (a New Jersey church for Binghamton's Christ Church), its "hours of operation" lines are usually a food pantry's, and its text can be old: check the address it prints. The Orthodox Church in America's parish pages (`oca.org/parishes/...`) give address and service times; the Maronite eparchy's `stmaron.org/parishes-n` a "Liturgical Schedule" for each parish; Quaker meetings are at `pym.org/meetings/meeting/<name>-friends-meeting/` (New Jersey and Pennsylvania; Salem Quarter on `southjerseyquakers.org`) and in New England Yearly Meeting's listings PDF; the United Methodist history commission's `gcah.org/landmarks/` pages give visiting times. The Church of Jesus Christ's location and historic-site pages (`churchofjesuschrist.org/learn/locations/...`, `/learn/historic-sites/...`) give weekly hours with `page.py --all` (`-g` misses them in a run-on line). `pcusa.org` and `rca.org` gave nothing for single congregations; a town's own site sometimes links a congregation. Read a diocesan PDF table with pdfplumber's word positions rather than pdftotext.
- **Look-alike sites** that a search offers first: presqueislelibrary.org (now a Wisconsin library), carylibrary.org (Lexington, Massachusetts), milfordhistoricalsociety.org (Milford, Connecticut, for Milford, Delaware), medfordhistorical.org (Massachusetts, for New Jersey), hebronhistoricalsociety.org (Connecticut), muuf.org (Morristown, New Jersey, for Middletown, New York), greenvillechurch.org (Westchester County), crossroadsumc.org (Pittsburgh, for Canton), oldfirstchurch.org (Tallahassee), fpccov.org (Covington, Georgia), stmcathedral.com (Halifax, for Galveston), mountholly.org (a church, for a cemetery), dio-bell.solutiosoftware.com (Belleville's diocese, for Evansville, Illinois), forestchurchpresbyterian (Forest, Virginia); library sites alexlibrary.net and leblib.org and warsawlibrary.org (Indiana), hutchpl.org and marysville.lib.nckls.org (Kansas), glenwood.lib.ia.us, marionpubliclibrary.org and norwalklibrary.org (Iowa), perrypubliclibrary.org (Ohio), lima.owwl.org (New York), milanlibrary.org (Michigan), lakewoodhistory.org (Ohio), littlefallshistoricalsociety.org (New York); Wikipedia's "Union City Public Library", "Monticello Carnegie Library" and "Spencer Public Library" are Indiana's. Searches also gave Elizabethtown, Kentucky's walking tour, Springfield, Missouri's "History Museum on the Square", Lodi, Ohio and Waterloo, Iowa societies, and churches of the same name in other states. Randolph College (Lynchburg) is not Randolph-Macon (Ashland). Watch towns that share names (Belleville, Lewistown, Chillicothe, Rockport, Shelbyville, Winchester); `hours.py page` prints the address line: read it before the times.
- **SAH Archipedia** (`sah-archipedia.org/node/<n>`, or `/buildings/<state>-<nn>-<county code><n>`) reads in full with `page.py --all`; the text sits between "Print Source" and "Writing Credits", and it gives present-day detail that 1970s Register forms lack. Each entry links its neighbours ("What's Nearby"): grep `href="/node/[0-9]*"` in the cached page to list a whole downtown at once. The `archipediavm.upress.virginia.edu` mirror reset the connection.
- **Clio** entries (`theclio.com/entry/<n>`) and tours (`theclio.com/tour/<n>`, stops at `/tour/<n>/<k>`) read in full with `page.py --all` (a whole tour with `-n 60000` or more) and with the web fetch tool. Entries often quote the Register form when the form will not open. Find an entry with a web search for the building's name and `theclio.com` (the search summary is not a source until the page is opened); entry numbers do not follow towns, and tour pages carry no links to the entries and no addresses.
- Commons photograph descriptions often quote the Register form (the Douglass School's in Lexington, Missouri), which gives size and style when the form itself cannot be read.
- `photo_find.py look` checks a pin: candidates "1,300 m, BY NAME ONLY" meant the pin was wrong (Shasta); photographs 0 to 6 m from the pin confirmed a Register point (Franklin School), 27 to 90 m in woods (Tishomingo, Grenada); one 137 m off found the right church (Holy Family, Eudora) and photographs far from the pin gave away a geocoder's mix of North and South (La Crosse). It is a lead finder too: it turned up a 1979 HAER picture of a ruined brick sugar mill at Laurel Valley that no article mentions.
- A place shows the photograph of the Wikipedia article its links name. Put the place's own article first; a "List of ..." link is passed over, so it is safe as the only Wikipedia link. A link to another building's article (a school district, a neighbouring cathedral, the federal building whose name Sioux City's old City Hall shares) brings that article's picture: link it with a plain `L()` link, write it as an `index.php` link (the Schomburg Center), or leave it out.
- **Waymarking** pages (`page.py --all`) give a position as `N 49° 38.019 W 114° 30.342` and often quote the interpretive sign; two of three such positions in Alberta were off the structure. They have a page for most Ohio and Indiana Carnegie libraries with architect, cost, materials and present use, mostly of about 2008 to 2010: a third-party source, so check present use elsewhere.
- The Pomeroy Foundation's marker pages (`wgpfoundation.org/historic-markers/<slug>`) carry a researched paragraph (often the village historian's), the address and a coordinate, more than HMdb's page for the same marker; some are drawn by script. Their coordinates can be off: 300 m (Beaver Falls Grange), 1 km (Gumaer Cemetery), 5 km (Homan-Gerard).
- `historypointer.com/nris/resource/<reference number>` gives the Register's street address where Wikipedia and NPGallery give none (Troy houses; the Colonie houses that Wikipedia's list shows as "address restricted" are ordinary addresses), carries Wikipedia's Register summary, and says whether a listing was removed. `page.py` calls its pages DRAWN BY SCRIPT, but `page.py --all -n 600` still prints the address and listing date (second line); some come back empty, and it put the Jamestown Armory in "Albany".
- The Wikipedia infobox's `location=` line in `research/wikiraw/` gives the street address for many houses whose list rows have none.
- New England: New England Carnegies (`necarnegies.com`, `ma<town>.htm`, `ct<town>.htm`) has a page for every Carnegie library with grant, architect, style and dates (present use as of about 2005); Buildings of New England (`buildingsofnewengland.com`) has dated posts with photographs and present use; Lost New England (`lostnewengland.com`) reads cleanly and gives demolition dates and present use.
- Cinema Treasures pages read well with `page.py -g` and give the fates of lost theatres with dates, often in the comments (Peoria, Zanesville).
- Ticket Tailor event pages (`tickettailor.com/events/<org>/<n>`) read cleanly and give tour start times. Lighthouse Friends pages (`lighthousefriends.com/light.asp?ID=<n>`) give a position, parking and the owner under "Visitor Information"; they are a write-up, so hours from them are not the owner's.
- The vanished-places list can hold a building that still stands under another use: "Philip Schuyler Elementary School" (the 1913 Albany High School), "Public Bath Number 2" in Albany, and "Ward School Number 2" in Norwich (the county museum).
- `nom.py` found street addresses in Independence, Missouri where it found no house or theatre by name: give it the address.
- **To check a county's Register list against the map:** `tools/nrhp_state.py "<STATE>" <key>` (or `tools/wd_nrhp.py <key>`) gives each listing with a point and works from a cloud session; match it by distance to `build/sites.json` (300 to 500 m). A distance test alone hides places (a district pin within 250 m made twenty Lawrence houses look covered), and a name test alone gives false "missing" results because ids and names differ, while word matches give false hits (Hyde Park, Chatham Square): grep `tools/data_*.py` for a distinctive word (the first two words; the plain surname, since apostrophes are escaped as `O\'Bannon`) and for the id before writing. Before writing up a big site, grep `build/sites.json` for an `lm-` pin of it (`start.py`'s 15 km did not reach `lm-olana` from Philmont).
- Wikipedia's category pages (`Category:Buildings_and_structures_in_<place>`, read with curl and a plain user agent, through the API or with `page.py`) list articles that a Register list lacks. The API answers 429 when asked in a row: leave about 8 seconds and retry; its geosearch is throttled too (run it in the background).
- With `choose.py --thinnest` giving a whole state, `start.py` lists nothing statewide: give a central town with a radius of 400 km.
- `note.py` makes no note for a batch with no data file: a video record with no places needs its note written by hand.
- Running `pincheck.py <id> --lidar` after the main run overwrites `research/pincheck_picked_1.jpg`: look at the sheets first.
- Plain Python `urllib` in a cloud container needs `SSL_CERT_FILE=/root/.ccr/ca-bundle.crt` (`hours.py` uses its own fetcher).
- A place list may hold small helpers (`def HM(...)`, `def NR(...)`); `safe_data.py` lets them through.
- A pull request opened as a draft does not merge by itself when it is later marked ready: `check.yml` runs on GitHub's default `pull_request` events (opened, synchronize, reopened), so marking it ready starts no run, and the run made while it was a draft skipped the merge job. The next push to the branch runs it again. (Not changed here: it is the workflow, which the owner looks after.)
- **LiDAR sweeps:** the 1,800 m map panel on a sheet is about 4.5 m to the pixel, so a building mark is 3 pixels; enlarging the middle of the panel tells lettering, town-line dashes and building squares apart. The building finder took a town-line dash for a building twice in Exeter and the sheet margin once in Monroe. `merge_from` in `lidar.py` compares only the date, so a verdict turned from "maybe" to "no" on the same day as the repository's copy is undone when `publish.sh` folds the ledger (bca2, bca3, pfca9; mark them again). `sweep "Reed (James A.) Memorial Wildlife Area"`, and once `"Bonanza Conservation Area"`, said NO SUCH UNIT though `find` prints them; Bonanza worked with `--state mo`. (These three are faults in the LiDAR tools, not changed here.) `lidar_dem.dem()` and `shade()` can be called directly for a wider bare-ground view than the pin-check tile.

### Alabama
- The Alabama Historical Commission's `ahc.alabama.gov/<Site>PlanYourVisit.aspx` pages read cleanly and give hours for each state historic site; other ahc.alabama.gov property pages answered 404 or timed out for another session.
- Refusing `page.py` (404 or time-out): huntsvillealabama.gov, oakleighmuseum.com, bhamwiki.com (403, archive copies unfetchable). Web search results quote the pages, and the Encyclopedia of Alabama is a reliable fallback.
- Addresses gone bad: www.dauphinisland.org is now a gambling spam page (Fort Gaines hours are on `townofdauphinisland.org/fort-gaines`); slossfurnaces.com does not answer (the museum is slossfurnaces.org); sturdivanthall.com is a hosting placeholder (the Encyclopedia of Alabama gives the house's hours and owner).
- Birmingham: Wikipedia has no article for several buildings (Alabama Power Building, Lyric Theatre, Tutwiler Hotel); go straight to SAH Archipedia or the Encyclopedia of Alabama.
- The Muscle Shoals National Heritage Area pages (msnha.una.edu) read cleanly and give current closures and directions for northwest Alabama (Old State Bank closed for restoration; Forks of Cypress directions).
- The Register list point for Fendall Hall, Eufaula, is 27 km west of the house; `nom.py`'s OpenStreetMap building was right. Check Barbour County list points.
- `start.py` has one region code (al) for the whole state, and `parking.py` finds almost nothing in rural Alabama.

### Alaska
- Juneau's managing pages read well with `page.py --all`: dnr.alaska.gov state park pages, juneau.org (museum hours, cemetery, newsroom notices), stnicholasjuneau.org, shrineofsainttherese.org (its FAQ page `?p=2890` has visiting hours).
- Alaska State Parks pages (`dnr.alaska.gov/parks/aspunits/southeast/...`) give access and fees but no hours for the Sitka units.

### Alberta
- Government of Alberta historic sites each have their own site (`<sitename>.ca/visit`: rutherfordhouse.ca, stephanssonhouse.ca, victoriasettlement.ca, historicdunvegan.ca, brooksaqueduct.ca) that `page.py --all` reads cleanly; historicdunvegan.ca also keeps an old "site closure" page that contradicts its visit page.
- The Edmonton and Calgary list titles are "List of historic places in the Edmonton Capital Region" and "...in the Calgary Region"; "...in Edmonton" and "...in Calgary" are redirects with no rows. Read them from `research/wikiraw/` by splitting on `{{HPC row`.
- Register coordinates (and Wikipedia's Alberta lists, which copy them) are often wrong: an island in the Athabasca, a housing estate, open fields; 500 to 750 m off for Rowley, Turner Valley and Waterton; empty fields for Bad Heart and Szypenitz. `nom.py` found the Turner Valley Gas Plant, and `look.py` at 250 m then showed it.
- `nom.py` knows almost nothing in rural Alberta, and `look.py` at 700 m could not pick out a church in Fort Chipewyan. `geo.py` put "753 Kettles Street, Lundbreck" in Pincher Creek as a PointAddress scoring 87: check the town in its answer.
- Refusing or gone: canmoremuseum.com's barracks page (404; the main page gives no barracks hours), flagstaff.ab.ca pages from search results (404); albertawow.com now serves a casino page.

### Arizona
- Arizona State Parks: `azstateparks.com/<park>/explore/facility-information` gives hours, holiday hours and fees in one page with `page.py --all`, and the park's `things-to-do/<name>` pages carry closures (the Audrey headframe). Slugs are not always the park name (Tubac Presidio is `azstateparks.com/tubac/...`); the Yuma prison has no facility page there, and `yumaprison.org/admission/` gives its hours.
- Refusing `page.py` (403 or 404): sharlothallmuseum.org (no archived copy; web search gave only directories for its hours), mohavemuseum.org, sanxaviermission.org, heritagesquarephx.org.
- Wikipedia coordinates for Montezuma Castle and the Douglas Mansion fell on roads; `osmnear.py ... -g 'viewpoint'` found the trail viewpoint below the castle.

### Arkansas
- Arkansas nomination forms on NPGallery (`_text`) had text layers, including the 1985 Stone County multiple-resource forms; `page.py -g` on them gives building dates, materials and owners in one call.
- encyclopediaofarkansas.net addresses guessed from a title answer 404 with no archived copy; take the exact address from a web search result first.
- Older Arkansas forms give a UTM reference, not latitude and longitude (`tools/utm.py`); the Brewer School's Register list coordinate was about 200 m off, in a field.
- Hot Springs: the NPS hours page (`nps.gov/hosp/planyourvisit/hours.htm`) says who runs each bathhouse now and links their own sites; `nps.gov/hosp/learn/historyculture/<name>-bathhouse.htm` has each bathhouse's history.
- Arkansas State Parks pages list several buildings' hours one after another, and `page.py -g` loses which line belongs to which building: read the cached copy in `research/pages/` around the word "Hours".
- Refusing `page.py`: stlukeshs.org (connection reset), mysticozark.com (403), washcohistoricalsociety.org, uafs.edu; a web search result quoting them was the only source for hours.

### British Columbia
- BC Parks pages (`bcparks.ca/<park>-park/`) read in full with `page.py --all` and say who maintains a structure (the Alexandra Bridge is the Ministry of Transportation's, not the park's).
- Wikipedia's "List of National Historic Sites of Canada in British Columbia" gives each site's Register id in a `{{CRHP|id|name}}` template: grep the cached raw text for `CRHP|`.
- Register points were on the building in Kaslo for most places (the S.S. Moyie's was 86 m off in trees) but fell on parking lots or in trees elsewhere (Barkerville, Cottonwood House, Fort St. James 570 m off). `nom.py "<site name>"` put Fort St. James, the Nanaimo Bastion, the Yale church and Craigflower Manor on the right building; it found nothing for the Keremeos Grist Mill, the Twin Falls Tea House, the Hastings Mill or Father Pandosy museums (the ArcGIS geocoder found 2691 Upper Bench Road, and `osmnear.py <lat> <lon> 150 buildings` found the Hastings Mill building from the park's Wikipedia point).
- West Kootenay: the FETCH Kootenay Boundary directory (`kb.fetchbc.ca/service.html?i=N`, updated 2026) lists museums with hours. For Anglican churches, `kootenayanglican.ca/west-kootenay-region/<church>` pages print service times and addresses cleanly.
- Refusing or gone: newdenver.ca (403, no usable archive copy); hatcreekranch.ca, the old Hat Creek Ranch address search results still give, now carries gambling spam, and the ranch's current historichatcreek.ca did not answer `page.py`.

### California
- California State Parks pages are `https://www.parks.ca.gov/?page_id=<number>`, read in full with `page.py --all` (hours, fees, description): 457 Weaverville Joss House, 456 Shasta, 509 Bodie, 552 Columbia, 499 Empire Mine, 449 Fort Ross, 485 Sutter's Fort, 497 Old Sacramento, 498 Governor's Mansion. Check the title line: 603 was Refugio State Beach, not La Purisima.
- The Los Angeles Archdiocese's parish pages (`lacatholics.org/venue/<parish>/`) give Mass times by day and language; the Diocese of Sacramento's archive PDFs on scd.org read well.
- San Diego: the Register rows are in "National Register of Historic Places listings in San Diego County, California" (no city list); "List of Gaslamp Quarter historic buildings" gives each building's address, year and a coordinate.
- Fresno: historicfresno.org reads cleanly: `nrhp/<name>.htm` for Register pages (names in `nrhp/nrhp.htm`), `lrhr/<street>.htm` and `lrhr/<number>.htm` for the Local Register, and `tours/nrhp.htm` (36,000 characters, `-n 40000`) with an address, paragraph and directions for every Register site in the city.
- Downtown addresses: ArcGIS PointAddress lookups landed on the building in Fresno, Sacramento (13 of 13, though the Carnegie library's fell on the newer tower behind it) and San Diego (`geo.py "<number street>, San Diego, CA 92101"`). Wikipedia gives Fresno's Bank of Italy and San Joaquin Light and Power building the same point; the Register point for San Diego's Park Place church is in Balboa Park, 110 m off.
- Tahoe National Forest: guessed addresses under `fs.usda.gov/r05/tahoe/recreation/...` all answered 404, and web search found no Kyburz Flat or Sierra Buttes page.
- Refusing `page.py` and the web fetch tool (403/406, or 404): angelsflight.org, sdcathedral.org, sdcatholic.org, gaslampfoundation.org, sandiegotheatres.org, stfrancisparish.com, saclibrary.org/locations/central (404).

### Colorado
- Register list titles: Denver's is "National Register of Historic Places listings in downtown Denver" ("...in Denver" and "...in Denver County, Colorado" have no rows). For small counties (Bent, Baca, Costilla) `wiki_raw.py --rows` prints a combined "listings in Colorado" table of 117 rows from several counties: grep it for the town names.
- historycolorado.org Register pages (`/node/<n>`) and `/location/<slug>` pages are mostly drawn by script and give a line or two; some give a paragraph (Bromide kilns; `node/56148` for Our Lady of Guadalupe, Conejos, which has no Wikipedia article although the county list names one). The Fort Garland museum page there reads fine and gives hours.
- Pueblo's cathedral is `shcathedral.net` (`shcathedral.org` is Knoxville, Tennessee). steelworks.us gives 403 to `page.py` but reads with the web fetch tool.
- argomilltour.com reads with `page.py` (argomillandtunnel.com returns nothing) but gives no tour times; taboroperahouse.net has no /tours or /visit page.
- Refusing `page.py`: prisonmuseum.org (resets), cpr.org (403; the Alpine Tunnel road reopening article too), hoteldeparismuseum.org (refused), denverfirefightersmuseum.org (resets; 503 to the web fetch tool).

### Connecticut
- State park pages (CT DEEP) read well with `page.py --all`. The hours sit in a line that begins "Details Open Daily", which the `-g` filters miss: search the `--all` text for "Sunset".
- New Haven County: the Madison Land Conservation Trust's pages and the regional council's trail brochures (`scrcog.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/<Name>.pdf`) both read cleanly and cover most town and land-trust trails in the county, with parking and ruins marked.
- CT DEEP park pages are at `portal.ct.gov/deep/state-parks/parks/<name>`; for state forests the working address was `ctparks.com/parks/<name>` (the `/forests/` form answered 404). Old Furnace State Park's DEEP page gives no hours; the Killingly Conservation Commission's leaflet (`killinglyconservation.org/_files/ugd/f1f6b2_e011d001a1e243b7befc3159fb8071d3.pdf`) has a dated history of the furnace and the park address.
- DEEP's "Seedling letterbox" clue pages (`portal.ct.gov/deep/forestry/letterboxing/...`) describe ruins along a forest trail step by step; they gave the only account of the cheese-box factory on the Henry Buck Trail.
- Drawn by script or refusing `page.py`: millmuseum.org, huntingtonhomestead.org, glastonburyct.gov (403), ctmq.org (resets), ctrivermuseum.org (404, no archive). CTvisit's listings (ctvisit.com) read cleanly and give hours, fees and days for the same places; savecottonhollowmill.org and Patch carried Cotton Hollow.
- HMdb's marker pages for the Dividend Pond Archaeological District, Rocky Hill, give each mill's story, what is left and a position; start at `https://www.hmdb.org/m.asp?m=301402`.
- The ArcGIS geocoder put "Pine Street & Elm Street, Manchester, CT" in Manchester, England; give a house number and ZIP (`geo.py` now keeps a ", CT" address in Connecticut).
- Register list coordinates in Old Saybrook (Lynde Point Light, the Shore Line power house) and Old Wethersfield fell 60 to 70 m off the buildings; the Solomon Rockwell House point in Winsted is in woods 50 m west of the house.
- Near the Rapallo Viaduct, `parking.py` offers a school lot on Smith Street; DEEP names the Air Line trailhead as "Smith Street, near Flanders Road". Look before pinning.
- New Haven: the Yale New Haven Building Archive (`nhba.yale.edu/building?id=N`, ids from search results) reads well with `page.py --all` and gives owner, year and architect. The Catholic parish site (newhavencatholic.org) is drawn by script, and stmarysnewhaven.org is now a spam site: do not link it.
- hillstead.org and lakecompounce.com answer 403; CTvisit listings (`ctvisit.com/listings/<name>`) give hours, fees and address for most Hartford County museums.

### Delaware
- destateparks.com pages read well with `page.py` (`/History/FortDelaware`, `/History/FortDupont`, `/History/AuburnValley`, `/WhiteClayCreek`, `/Lums`); guessed addresses such as `/LumsPond` answer 404. New Castle County's own park pages (newcastlede.gov) answered 403.
- `history.delaware.gov/historic-sites/` lists every state-owned site run by others (Abbott's Mill, Belmont Hall, the Breakwater and Fenwick lights, Fort Christina, Old Sussex Courthouse, Prince George's Chapel and more) with an address, phone and a line on visiting. The state museum pages (`/old-state-house/`, `/john-dickinson-plantation/`, `/zwaanendael-museum/`, `/coochs-bridge-historic-site`) read cleanly; `/new-castle-court-house/` saves a PNG and prints nothing, and the working address is `history.delaware.gov/history.delaware.gov/new-castle-court-house-museum` (the doubled host is real).
- The Public Archives' marker pages (`archives.delaware.gov/delaware-historical-markers/<name>`) read with `page.py --all -n 3000`; the plain `-g` filter printed nearly nothing.
- `newcastlehistory.org/plan-your-visit` lists several sites' hours on one page; Amstel House's lines are those under "Amstel House tours start on even hours".
- Episcopal: episcopalassetmap.org node 2664, offered for Christ Church Dover, is a Methodist page; the Diocese's list (`delaware.church/episcopal-churches-in-delaware/`) gives each parish's address. christchurchdover.org reset the connection.
- Kent and Sussex Register points were off the building for Christ Church Dover, Parson Thorne and St. George's Chapel (in a pond); the ArcGIS PointAddress and `nom.py` landed on the building.
- The fws.gov Bombay Hook visit page answers 404; the Friends of Bombay Hook site reads.

### District of Columbia
- The DC Register lists are "... in Northeast Quadrant, Washington, D.C.", "... in Southeast Quadrant, Washington, D.C.", "... in central Washington, D.C.", "... in the upper NW Quadrant of Washington, D.C." and "... in western Washington, D.C."; the plain DC title has no rows.
- The Capitol Visitor Center's hours are at `visitthecapitol.gov/visit/directions-and-hours` (the older plan-visit addresses are 404).
- Refusing `page.py`: Dumbarton House and Planet Word (resets); the Postal Museum's pages, dar.org and oas.org (403 to the web fetch tool as well).

### Florida
- Florida State Parks pages (every park page and the hours-fees page) answer 403 to `page.py`. The brochure PDFs under `floridastateparks.org/sites/default/files/media/file/` read, and the system guide booklet there (`FSPSystemGuide_Booklet_ForReview-1.pdf`) has a paragraph per park with its address and the fee rule, and hours for some (Dudley Farm, the Rawlings home, San Marcos, Gamble, Bulow, The Barnacle); it is the operator, so usable.
- Read in full: ringling.org/visit/help, edisonford.org (front-page footer), coralcastle.com/visit, kwahs.org/visit/<museum>, plantmuseum.com/discover/visit-accessibility. visitstaugustine.com listings give hours and fees for St. Augustine attractions whose own sites refuse, but it is the county visitor bureau, so those hours stay out of `hours=`.
- Refusing `page.py`: vizcaya.org, flaglermuseum.us/visit, deeringestate.org/visit (a web search quoted the hours).

### Georgia
- Georgia State Parks pages (gastateparks.org) read with `page.py --all`; the hours are one run of text after "Hours." (park, office, historic site, last tour), which `-g` cuts, so use `page.py --all <url> | grep -oE '.{0,150}Hours.{0,350}'`. Addresses that worked: `TravelersRest`, `JarrellPlantation`, `LaphamPattersonHouse`, `HighFalls`, `HofwylBroadfieldPlantation` (the hyphenated one is 404).
- Vanishing Georgia (vanishinggeorgia.com) posts read in full with a photograph each; `brick.py look <id> <picture address>` takes the `wp-content/uploads/...jpg` in the cached page. The Scull Shoals post's picture was the river, not the ruins.
- Refusing `page.py`: exploregeorgia.org (403; the state listings with hours for many small sites; archived copies not tried), chsgeorgia.org (403; State Railroad Museum, Savannah History Museum), foxtheatre.org/tours (406; the home page gives box-office times).

### Hawaii
- State park pages (`dlnr.hawaii.gov/dsp/parks/oahu/<slug>/`) read with `page.py`, but the `-g` summary cuts the winter hours: use `--all` and search for "Winter:". Every page ends with the same statewide closure notices, which match most patterns: cut the text off at "**IMPORTANT PARK NOTICES".
- Oahu: the HMdb nearby list returned the same Pearl Harbor markers whatever the point; it was not useful for finding ruins there.
- Oahu: National Register list coordinates were good for the heiau and the lighthouses but 400 m off for Keaīwa Heiau (the point is in forest on private watershed land) and 450 m off for Battery Randolph. Wikipedia's point for Heʻeia Fishpond lands on houses.
- The Painted Church's old address (`thepaintedchurch.org`) now holds an unrelated Spanish-language blog: take no hours from it. `imiolachurch.com` did not answer, nor its archived copy.
- The Kona Historical Society said in November 2026 it must raise $200,000 by December 31, 2026 to stay open: check its places' hours again in 2027.
- `nom.py` found most heiau and churches in Kona by name, but not Huliheʻe Palace, Kahikolu Church or the Kohala courthouse; the ArcGIS geocoder's POI for "Kohala District Courthouse" was the South Kohala court in Waimea.
- The closure notices at the bottom of the state park pages are the same on every page and name other parks (the Kealakekua page carried Lapakahi's reopening date): read them for the whole island.
- The Pāʻulaʻula page's own construction closure notice (March 2025) sits at the top, above "Hours", and the `-g` summary drops it: read with `--all`.
- `nom.py` did not find Russian Fort Elizabeth, Piʻilanihale, Kīkīaola, Makawao Union Church, the Molokaʻi Museum or Kaunolū by name.
- lymanmuseum.org answers 403 (the archived copy read). The kulacatholiccommunity.org Mass page mixes two churches: the Holy Ghost times follow "HOLY GHOST MISSION".

### Idaho
- `history.idaho.gov/franklin/` and `history.idaho.gov/stricker/` give hours, owner and address with `page.py --all`. The Idaho Architecture Project (`idahoarchitectureproject.org/properties/<slug>/`) gives current use and surviving fabric.
- Boise: the city's Julia Davis Park bollard pages (`cityofboise.org/.../decorative-bollards/<name>/`) settle what became of lost buildings (City Hall, the Natatorium, St. Teresa's Academy, the Ada County Courthouse).
- Register forms tried from 1974 to 1999 (Shoshone County, Boise, Idaho City) had text layers; the 2021 listings (100007006, 100007007) and Grand Forks have almost none. HMdb is dense round Idaho City and Boise's North End: one nearby list gave positions for most of Idaho City.
- The ArcGIS geocoder gave PointAddress results for most street addresses; the Paris and Rock Creek ones were close to but not on the building.
- canyoncounty.id.gov park pages are 404; the BLM's Celebration Park page covers the Guffey bridge. spokanehistorical.org reads (not the `mail.` subdomain search results give).
- Refusing `page.py`: visitidaho.org, cdapress.com (403), inlander.com (429), rexburgid.gov ("Client Challenge"), visitpocatello.com ("please wait"), chesterfieldfoundation.org (no connection), idahocitychamber.org (resets), idahocityhistoricalfoundation.org (drawn by script; museum hours were found only quoted on Clio); `parksandrecreation.idaho.gov/parks/land-yankee-fork/info` is 404.

### Illinois
- The greatriversgreenway.org page for the Chain of Rocks Bridge hours is 404 with no archived copy; `nps.gov/places/chain-of-rocks-bridge.htm` gives parking and "open daily".
- A Mississippi bridge pin from OpenStreetMap can fall on the Missouri side: pin the Illinois bank end.
- HMdb's nearby list round Elsah, Grafton and Alton gave the Elsah ice house ruin and the Clifton Terrace winery, which no Register list names.
- Illinois DNR historic sites: one session found every `dnrhistoric.illinois.gov/experience/sites/...` address answering with the Albany Mounds page (no hours readable, web searches gave titles only); a later one found each site page at `dnrhistoric.illinois.gov/experience/sites/site.<slug>.html` gives an "Open:" line under its address (the list page is drawn by script; ignore the JSON block holding every site).
- Shaw Local's 2025 survey of Illinois Carnegie libraries covers many towns in one page.
- historicelgin.com (the Elgin Heritage Commission's plaque site) names pages by street address (`105-n-gifford-street`); the list is in `https://historicelgin.com/scripts/maps.js`.
- peoriahistoricalsociety.com did not answer `page.py` or the web fetch tool (503) on November 24, 2026.

### Indiana
- indianamuseum.org: a wrong slug under `/historic-sites/<slug>/plan-your-visit/` quietly serves the T. C. Steele page. Slugs that work: corydon-capitol-historic-site, lanier-mansion-historic-site, vincennes-state-historic-site (also gives Fort Knox II's outdoor hours), levi-and-catharine-coffin-state-historic-site, whitewater-canal-historic-site, tc-steele-historic-site, limberlost-state-historic-site, new-harmony-historic-site, culbertson-mansion-historic-site. Read the hours with `--all` and grep "Plan your visit to".
- Forms: the SHAARD PDFs Wikipedia cites (`secure.in.gov/apps/dnr/shaard/...`) return only the database's front page; use NPGallery's `_text`. NPGallery's 1982 Indiana listings (downtown Evansville) are two-page inventory sheets, mostly handwriting; the typed "Historical Information" paragraph gives address, architect and year.
- Carnegie libraries: the Indiana State Library blog post, the state historical marker pages (`in.gov/history/state-historical-markers`; dates, architects, materials) and small libraries' own `in.gov/<library>/about` pages.
- Indianapolis: the Indiana War Memorials home page `in.gov/iwm/index.html` is 404 (`page.py` falls back to a January 2026 copy); the tours page (`in.gov/iwm/visitor-information/tours-and-reservations/`) gives museum and monument hours, which disagree with the home page about the monument. crownhill.org is 403; `crownhillhf.org/visit/` gives the gate hours. sjoa.org and archindy.org are 403, but archived copies read (the `/mass-times` copy gave Mass times though `page.py` called it drawn by script).
- The pin check's land map holds Indiana state parks, historic sites and the Hindostan Falls fishing area, but not the Whitewater Canal site at Metamora.
- Refusing `page.py`: allencountycourthouse.org, fwhistorycenter.org (403, archive unfetchable), fwembassytheatre.org sub-pages (404; the home page lists only shows), historymuseumsb.org (403), Indiana DNR's Mansfield Roller Mill page (404 on www and secure).

### Iowa
- Iowa DNR park pages and history.iowa.gov give hours plainly with `page.py --all`. The State Historical Society's site pages print the Des Moines museum's hours first: read the "Visiting Information" part.
- Davenport's list is split into "Downtown Davenport", "east Davenport" and "west Davenport" (lowercase east and west); the city title has no rows.
- Encyclopedia Dubuque (`encyclopediadubuque.org/index.php/<NAME_IN_CAPITALS>`) follows buildings to the present (the 2024 brewery demolition, Steeple Square, the 1916 Wartburg buildings).
- The Sioux City Public Museum's walking-tour leaflets (`siouxcitymuseum.org/s/Walking-tour-<Street>-Street-2020-update.pdf`) say which downtown buildings were razed and when.
- Carnegie libraries: carnegielibrariesiowa.org has a page for almost every town at `/library/<town>/` (architect, contractor, dates; no wall material); the web fetch tool reads it, curl does not, and its dated openings often differ from Wikipedia's by a year. Most libraries have a site at `http://www.<town>.lib.ia.us/` with hours on the home page.
- oldcap.uiowa.edu is drawn by script and gives no hours. pellahistorical.org and the Surf Ballroom read fine.
- Refusing: vesterheim.org, newmelleray.org, cathedralstpats.org (403), capitoltheatreqc.com (406), stlukesumcdbq.org and dubuquecounty.gov (no connection).

### Kansas
- **The register search.** The Kansas Historical Society's search (`kansashistory.gov/natreg_listings/search/page:N/county:XX/records:all`) gives a real summary for every listing, State Register ones too, and the address of each nomination PDF (it answers `curl` too; the PDFs follow `resource/national_register/nominationsNRDB/<name>.pdf`); many listings have no Wikipedia article behind their row, so it was the best Kansas source. County codes are two letters: WY Wyandotte (6 pages of ten), JF Jefferson (2), MI Miami (its fifth page would not come through), SG Sedgwick (16), FO Ford, SW Seward, DG Douglas (about 13), JO Johnson. **On November 26, 2026 `.../page:1/county:JO/records:all` answered with an error page (the site changed), so this may no longer work.**
- Reading those pages: `page.py` cuts them and `-g` keeps only sentences it likes. Use `--all -n 40000` (`-n 20000` for ten summaries), or strip the HTML saved in `research/pages/` with a few lines of Python, or fetch with plain `requests`/curl. The records sit in one long line: split on "Register Nomination Inventory Record", "Inventory Record .", "National Register Nomination" or "Listed in National Register", or keep the text between "starting on record" (or "Records:") and "New Search"; the rest is menus. The name sits two lines above the county line. Pages past the last repeat it.
- **Forms.** NPGallery's text (`https://npgallery.nps.gov/NRHP/GetAsset/NRHP/<number>_text`) read for every form from 1972 to about 2008, even where the Society's own PDF of the same form is a scan: try it before calling a form unread. Numbers starting 100 (listed after 2016) return a small PNG, not text. The Society keeps those as PDFs under `/resource/national_register/nominationsNRDB/`; the link names are in the page's HTML, not in `page.py`'s text (pull them with a short `requests` call, or a web search for the name finds them). They read well with `pypdf` (`page.py -g .` shows only a few lines); State Register nominations and forms from before about 2000 there are mostly scans.
- Commons has the forms' own photographs for 2020s listings, geotagged and named for the property (Dcomputerman12, SharonPapierdreams; one contributor did all of Douglas County, another the whole Leavenworth list, SharonPapierdreams most of downtown Manhattan by address), so `photo_find.py` finds pictures for farms and barns with no article, and they settle red brick. Their descriptions quote the nomination text, so `photo_find.py look` doubles as a way to read a form NPGallery serves only as an image (it cuts each description short).
- **Johnson County is worked out at the Register level** (after four batches only scanned State Register nominations remain). Next sources: the 2009 county survey report (`https://www.kansashistory.gov/resource/survey/jocosurveyreport2009.pdf`, 160 pages with a text layer; `page.py --all` stops at 12,000 characters, so download it and run `pdftotext -layout`), which names dozens of stone and frame buildings by address in Lenexa, De Soto, Shawnee, Overland Park and Gardner; the Clio driving tour `https://theclio.com/tour/1012` (28 stops in Olathe, Overland Park, Shawnee, Merriam); Lenexa Historical Society pages (`lenexahistoricalsociety.org/historic-locations`, written from dated newspapers, Old Town buildings no list has); Johnson County Post (johnsoncountypost.com, had the Walker School story); city survey PDFs and local histories; or the Kansas Historic Resources Inventory. Or go to another county (Miami and Franklin were started).
- Several Kansas state historic sites close for the winter (early October to April); the KSNT/Yahoo story "Seven Kansas historic sites to close in October" lists which stay open year-round.
- Web search could not confirm several small listings (Oskaloosa church, Delaware Cemetery) and often finds only real-estate pages for a registered house; the register summary is then the only source.
- Wyandotte County's Register list is all on the map or in a leads table: a further Kansas City, Kansas session should use Clio's Kansas City, Kansas tours and the city's landmark list (wycokck.org answers 403 to `page.py`). Leavenworth, Johnson and Wyandotte lists are nearly all on the Atlas; a session there should use the Society's survey reports and Fort Leavenworth's own pages.
- State historic sites: the short addresses `kansashistory.gov/<site>` (fort_hays, hollenberg, kaw_mission, mine_creek) give hours, address and season with `page.py --all`; cottonwood_ranch and goodnow_house do not, but their "plan your visit" pages (found by web search) do.
- Carnegie libraries: the group nomination (NPGallery 64000195; its PDFs read with curl) lists the listed buildings with architects and names the 13 torn down and those too altered to list.
- Leavenworth: the city's district pages (`leavenworthks.gov/cd/page/<name>-historic-district`: Downtown, Third Avenue, Arch Street, North Broadway, South Esplanade, Union Park) describe buildings one by one with dates; save the output to a file, as the page cache is keyed by hash. A web search finds the Army's Fort Leavenworth wayside leaflet PDF, a paragraph a stop. The list point for the county courthouse is far off: check each Leavenworth list point.
- Manhattan: K-State building pages (`k-state.edu/maps/buildings/<letter>/`: F, C, N, A; Holtz Hall is HZ) give a short dated history. The Downtown Manhattan Historic District form (NPGallery 06001240_text, 86 pages) has a numbered entry per building: search it for "<number>. <name> Status".
- Pins: the ArcGIS address lookup (geocode.arcgis.com, no key; `countryCode=USA`) gave PointAddress at score 100 on the building for every Lawrence, Eudora and Leavenworth street address tried, and for Grinter Place, Castle Rock, a library and a Harvey house where the Register and Wikipedia points were 70 to 270 m off; `nom.py` found only some. But in Wichita it put 430 N Broadway (the cathedral) and 2100 W University (Friends) on the wrong building where OpenStreetMap was right; a rural address can return two points or a house in another county (Delaware Cemetery): check the county and look at each. A form's boundary points can fall in a lake (Clinton).
- Refusing `page.py` (403 unless noted): mahaffie.org, lenexa.com (the web fetch tool read it, and gardnerhistoricalmuseum.com), jocogov.org departments (403/404), olatheks.gov, jocohistory.org, ecatholic parish sites, fumclawrence.org, oldcowtown.org (and to the web fetch tool), ksoutdoors.gov and .com (and to the web fetch tool), wichita.edu (an Incapsula block page), home.army.mil (502; only web search text for Fort Leavenworth).

### Kentucky
- parks.ky.gov is drawn by script (`page.py` gets 63 characters) and its static mirror answers 403; a local paper (the Harrodsburg Herald) carried the 2026 hours for Old Fort Harrod.
- McDowell House's site (`mcdowellhouse.com/constitution-square`) carries Constitution Square's hours too; Perryville Battlefield's read cleanly from `perryvillebattlefield.org/html/directions_and_hours.html`.
- parks.ky.gov stays unreadable. The Office of Kentucky Nature Preserves (`eec.ky.gov/Nature-Preserves/Locations/...`) lists some state historic sites with hours (it gave Old Mulkey's); My Old Kentucky Home has its own site, `visitmyoldkyhome.com`.
- Blue Heron: guessed `https://www.nps.gov/biso/...` names are 404; `home.nps.gov/biso/learn/historyculture/blueheron.htm` reads cleanly.
- Daniel Boone National Forest pages are at `fs.usda.gov/r08/danielboone/recreation/<name>-interpretive-site` (without `-interpretive-site` is 404) and give a coordinate, hours and directions.
- Louisville: the downtown list is "National Register of Historic Places listings in Downtown Louisville, Kentucky" (Union Station and the Water Tower are not on it). Louisville Water's visit page (`louisvillewater.com/louisville-water-tower/visit-us/`) reads in full (the old `/waterworks-museum/` is 404). The Derby Museum's hours are on `derbymuseum.org/visit-guide` (`plan-a-visit` holds only menu text). archlou.org (every parish page and the Mass schedule), cathedraloftheassumption.org and lfpl.org answer 403, and Mass-time searches lead only to directories; St. Boniface's `stbonifaceparish.com` reads.

### Louisiana
- `wiki_raw.py` found no article for Lincoln Beach (New Orleans); nola.gov and Axios New Orleans carried the facts. Its Register list point is inland of the levee; the beach is 400 m north on the lake side.
- In the coastal marsh the LiDAR sheet is worth reading even for standing masonry: it showed Fort Macomb where the aerial showed only trees.
- The first Wikipedia picture here was a drawing (Fort St. Philip), a map (Battery Bienvenue) or a postcard (the Mint): use `brick.py look --pic N` for a photograph.
- lastateparks.com (every state historic site page and press release) and explorelouisiana.com answer 403, and search results give titles only, so state historic site hours could not be read unattended. The nps.gov/places pages for Los Adaes and Fort Jesup read well and give the address and what visitors can see. The Germantown Colony Museum page on sos.la.gov is 404.
- The NPS Cane River pages warn that GPS sends visitors wrong: take parking from `parking.py` and the park's directions page.
- Catholic dioceses: the Diocese of Alexandria's parish pages (`diocesealex.org/church/...`) list each mission church's address and Mass times under its parent parish (read the whole page with `page.py -g`, not only `hours.py page`); the Diocese of Shreveport's (`dioshpt.org/church/...`) give them when the parish's own site answers 403 (Holy Trinity).
- downtownshreveport.com (the Downtown Development Authority) has short dated histories of lost and standing buildings.
- The ArcGIS geocoder found named places `nom.py` could not (Afton Villa Gardens, Germantown Colony Museum, Frogmore); Afton Villa gave two points 1.4 km apart.
- Refusing: stlouiscathedral.org, louisianastatemuseum.org (403; the web fetch tool read the museum pages), shreveportmunicipalauditorium.com (403); oldursulineconvent.org did not resolve from the cloud.

### Maine
- The state park day-use fee page (`maine.gov/DACF/parks/park_passes_fees_rules/park_day_use_fees.shtml`) reads with `page.py --all` and gives fees for every state historic site at once.
- The Maine Coastal Program's Coastal Public Access Guide PDFs (maine.gov/dmr, one for each stretch of coast, May 2026) read with `page.py` and give parking, dogs, fees and a coordinate for every shore park and fort.
- Refusing `page.py`: lincolncountyhistory.org (403; Lincoln County News, lcnme.com, and the Wiscasset Newspaper carry the museum dates), historicnewengland.org (403, archives unreadable), bristolmaine.org park pages (404; thefishermensmuseum.org gives the Pemaquid park season), alfredshakermuseum.com (drawn by script).
- Wikipedia coordinates in Lincoln County were often 30 to 200 m off (8 of 18 moved); the Perkins Tide Mill point in Kennebunkport is on a house 400 m from the property (the geocoder finds 8 Mill Lane).
- On the Salmon Falls River at South Berwick the state outline runs about 240 m east of the river, so a pin on Hamilton House sits outside Maine's outline (`check.py` now warns instead of refusing).
- `maine.gov/<fortknox|fortobrien|fortkent|fortpoint>` read well with `page.py --all` and give the season, fee and a state coordinate; the coordinate for Fort O'Brien and Fort Kent lands on a road and a bridge approach, and the address lookup was better.
- Aroostook: nearly every Register row says "no coordinates (often address restricted)" (about a third have a point), and none of the Aroostook or Downeast leads layers is rich in names.
- The three Lincoln County meetinghouses are opened in summer by local societies; their hours appear in the Lincoln County News and the Wiscasset Newspaper each June, not on any owner's page found.
- The Maine Historic Preservation Commission's architect biographies (maine.gov/mhpc) list each architect's buildings: good for who designed what.

### Manitoba
- The Manitoba Historical Society's "Historic Sites of Manitoba" pages (`https://mhs.mb.ca/docs/sites/<slug>.shtml`) read well with `page.py --all -n 30000` and end in a "Site Coordinates (lat/long): N.., W.." line (`grep -o` pulls a dozen at once). The slug is often guessable (`lowerfortgarry`, `rielhouse`); a wrong one returns a 609-character "MHS Page not found" (about half of guesses), and a web search for "mhs.mb.ca <site name>" finds the rest. Its "Abandoned Manitoba" page (`/docs/sites/abandoned.shtml`) links about 400 ruins, kilns, quarries, dams and bridges: grep its cached copy for `href="<slug>.shtml">`.
- The Society's pages can be ahead of the Province's (a store's changes, a tower burned in 2008, a store demolished in 2024 still listed as standing by the Province): read the Society's page before adding a provincial site. The Province's index (`gov.mb.ca/chc/hrb/prov/index.html`) lists every Provincial Heritage Site by area, each with a short page `.../prov/pNNN.html` giving owner and designation date.
- Society coordinates were off the building for 6 of 19 in one session (one in a street) and 2 of 17 in another (Lower Fort Garry on the riverbank, though its page lists each building's own point; Inglis on grass); `nom.py "<name>, <town>, Manitoba"` (or `osmnear.py <lat> <lon> 120 buildings` for Portage City Hall) gave the building each time.
- Commons has pictures for few small Manitoba sites (the Forks, Camp Hughes, Johnston Terminal, Pointe du Bois, the Brandon hangar as a postcard).
- The Ross House operator's page (sevenoakshouse.ca) answered 404; a web search gave only directory hours.

### Maryland
- DNR park pages read well at `dnr.maryland.gov/publiclands/Pages/western/<name>.aspx`, but the guessed name is often wrong (Cunningham Falls is `Cunningham.aspx`): search for the exact address.
- Frederick County Parks and Recreation (`recreater.com`) answered 404 or reset the connection.
- NPS Monocacy pages are at `nps.gov/mono/learn/historyculture/<name>.htm` with an underscore (`thomas_farm.htm`, `gambrill_mill.htm`).
- Several Wikipedia coordinates in Frederick County sat 50 to 550 m off (Catoctin Furnace's in woods south of the furnace); `osmnear.py ... buildings -g '<name>'` found the right building every time.
- DNR park pages give hours in a side box that only `page.py --all` shows ("9 a.m. to sunset" on the Patapsco page); `parkinfo.aspx?parkname=<park>` gives a street address for each historic building and area in a park and the hours by area. The Howard County museum page (`howardcountymd.gov/ecborail`) prints its hours only with `--all`.
- The Maryland State Parks markers on HMdb (Rock Run, Lapidum, Deer Creek, Stafford) quote the park's signs in full with a position; one nearby list round Rock Run gave nearly every Susquehanna State Park place.
- Baltimore: the Pratt library's location history page (`prattlibrary.org/about-us/history/location-history`) gives opening and closing dates for every branch, and Maryland Historical Trust form B-1387 (B-1384 inside) gives branch numbers and architects.

### Massachusetts
- duxburyhistory.org reads cleanly and gives season, tour days and parking for King Caesar House and the Bradford House. pilgrimjohnhowlandsociety.org answers 403.
- The Plymouth Antiquarian Society's 2026 Liberty Trail brochure PDF (plymouthantiquarian.org) gives street addresses for Revolutionary sites, Parting Ways at 287 Plympton Road among them.
- Register list titles: Worcester's is split in three ("... in northwestern / eastern / southwestern Worcester, Massachusetts"); Holyoke has none, so use `wiki_raw.py --rows -g Holyoke "National Register of Historic Places listings in Hampden County, Massachusetts"` (16 rows).
- Register coordinates were often off: five of seven in Berkshire County (Glendale, Monument Mills, Tyringham Shaker, Searles Castle, Old Covered Bridge), Elm Hill Farm and Warren Town Hall (in woods), Fall River (the high school and St. Patrick's in the street, the waterworks in North Watuppa Pond, Church of the Ascension 320 m off), Wamsutta Mills (on Route 18) and the Apponegansett Meeting House. ArcGIS PointAddress results landed on the building in Concord, New Bedford, Fairhaven and elsewhere, except Naumkeag (on its lot) and Chesterwood (in trees). Concord's OpenStreetMap Robbins House point is under tree shadow, with a bigger private house 90 m east that looks like the target on the aerial.
- Lowell: Doors Open Lowell's one-page histories (`https://doorsopenlowell.org/s/<Name>-DOL-2024.pdf`; take the link from a web search, guessed names fail) and the City Historic Board's page for each listed building (`lowellma.gov/984` lists them in its margin).
- Minute Man: the "places to go" page (`nps.gov/mima/planyourvisit/placestogo.htm`) gives each site's season hours and a GPS point for each parking lot; the hours page has no times. louisamayalcott.org reads only without "www" (the www address resets).
- New Bedford: the NPS hours page (`nps.gov/nebe/planyourvisit/hours.htm`) read "Page In-Progress" in November 2026; `rjdmuseum.org/visit/` carries the Rotch-Jones-Duff hours and fees (`/hours-and-admission` is drawn by script). The MassGIS aerial does not reach Butler Flats in the outer harbour.
- Salem: salemma.gov answered 404 or a reset for every address (no archived copy); Destination Salem (salem.org) carries hours for City-run sites. PEM's walking-tour page (`pem.org/visit/tours/salem-walking-tours`) is the only place found that says when the Gardner-Pingree and John Ward houses can be seen inside.
- westbrookfield.org (the Historical Commission) has a page for most sites in the town; its Old Indian Cemetery address is spelled "old-indian-cemetary".
- theberkshireedge.com reads cleanly but prints today's date, not the article's. The Historical Commission town report address searches give (`sec.state.ma.us/.../town-reports/sbd.pdf`) is 404.
- Refusing `page.py` (403): clintonchurchrestoration.org, sandisfieldartscenter.org, eqlt.org, brookfieldma.us, marbleheadma.gov, fairhaven-ma.gov, richardhowe.com, stjp2parish.org, cathedralofsaintpaul.net, and the Worcester diocese's ecatholic.com pages (also to the web fetch tool; search-result text is the only way in).

### Michigan
- SAH Archipedia building pages read in full with `page.py --all` at `https://sah-archipedia.org/buildings/MI-01-<code><n>`: Grand Rapids KT1 to about KT45; Kalamazoo KZ1 to KZ26; Washtenaw WA1 to WA24, with University of Michigan buildings WA7.1 to WA7.8 (Hill Auditorium 7.1, Burton Tower 7.2, Clements Library 7.6, Law Quadrangle 7.7).
- Ann Arbor: the District Library's "Historic Buildings" pages (`aadl.org/buildings_hhaaNNN`, `aadl.org/node/NNN`) give architect, dates and materials. aahom.org redirects to `discoverscienceandnature.org/aahom`, whose hours are drawn by script (the web fetch tool reads them).
- Detroit: HistoricDetroit.org pages say what happened to each building and when; the 1914 Detroit Public Library report on archive.org describes every early branch.
- Carnegie libraries: northernmichiganhistory.com has a page for nearly every northern one; the state marker texts are PDFs on the DNR site (www and www2 hosts), named by marker number.
- michigan.gov museum and DNR pages read well with `page.py --all`; the Iron Industry Museum's front page carries reopening notices the visitor page lacks (roof damage shut it from February to June 2, 2026). michigan.gov/mhc visitor pages answer 404, but the parent (`michigan.gov/mhc/museums/fwchl`) gives admission, though no hours.
- Shore lighthouses and forts: Wikipedia and Register points often fall in the lake (Pointe aux Barques, Au Sable, the Upper Peninsula); `nom.py` found the right point every time.
- Refusing `page.py`: kalamazoocity.org (403; the web fetch tool read City Hall's hours), kpl.gov (403), detroitmi.gov park pages, cityofnegaunee.com (403, archives unfetchable), the NPS Au Sable page and bigsablelighthouse.org (a web search quoted the 2026 season), detroitobservatory.umich.edu, clements.umich.edu, uunions.umich.edu, fccannarbor.org (403 to the web fetch tool too), muer.com (522).

### Minnesota
- MNHS's Marine Mill hours page comes back with the "Hours" heading empty (drawn by script). washingtoncountymn.gov/3089/Tours answers 404; the courthouse's hours are at washingtoncountymn.gov/3472.
- MNHS (`mnhs.org/<site>/visit/hours`), Minnesota DNR (`dnr.state.mn.us/state_parks/<park>/index.html`), Winona County Historical Society, Hibbing Mine View, Sheldon Theatre, Danebod and Crow Wing County pages are drawn by script; the headless-browser recipe under "Every state" read them all (DNR's tours.html timed out; faribaultcathedral.com and fmr.org time out on networkidle but still return text). fitgers.com's home page reads that way, but its history and parking pages stop at a "verifying your request" screen. MNHS's winter pages list every day "Closed" for seasonal houses: read the summer hours in the open season.
- Carnegie libraries: placeography.net has a page for nearly every one, razed ones included ("(Razed)" in the title), with addresses, architects and wall materials and colours.
- Duluth: mncourts.gov answers 403 and a Cloudflare check to a headless browser (the county's office list gives the courthouse's weekday hours); zenithcity.com pages come back as a 404 page.

### Mississippi
- First pass: `wiki_raw.py --rows -g` in one loop over all 82 county Register lists finds the sites with an article in a few minutes; SAH Archipedia (`sah-archipedia.org/node/<n>`) then gives the best "what survives" text.
- River towns: the HMdb nearby list round a town gave nearly every building's marker with its position in one request (Port Gibson's "Historic Tour" markers, Grand Gulf park, Rodney).
- Wikipedia and Register points for small-town buildings were off in 5 of 17 pins, and by 50 m or more at McRaven, King Edward and Brookhaven; the ArcGIS geocoder's address or POI hits (Waverly Mansion) were right each time.

### Missouri
- **Register forms.** Many sessions found no Missouri form readable: the old `mostateparks.com/sites/mostateparks/files/<Name>.pdf` addresses that web searches still list answer 404 (Douglass School, Wentworth, Cumberland Presbyterian, "Historic Resources of Lexington", Bailey Farm, Four Gates, the Lee's Summit and Grandview surveys), `dnr.mo.gov/shpo/nps-nr/<ref>.pdf` answers 503, and the archived copies cannot be fetched. **Later sessions found the forms moved:** `https://mostateparks.com/sites/g/files/zuston361/files/media/pdf/2025/02/<name>.pdf` (districts `<name>-hd.pdf`), found by a web search limited to that site; the Johnson County courthouse, Chilhowee and the Holden and Pine district read well, others are scans or 404. `page.py --all -n 400000` reads a whole one; the text is one line, so use `grep -o` with context.
- **NPGallery** serves a one-page placeholder (about 22 KB) for most Missouri forms from 1972 to about 2012 (Ray, Platte and Jackson counties, Kansas City; e.g. 82003142, 91001151, 77000809), with exceptions (79001372; Lake of the Forest, 1996; the 2013 Stoutimore and Mount Washington School forms). Text is readable for numbers 13xxxxxx to 16xxxxxx and 100xxxxx up to about 100008xxx (another session found the 100 range readable only to about 100001xxx); test with `page.py` before planning. Newer forms also come as full PDFs from the `npgallery.nps.gov/GetAsset/<uuid>` links a web search for "NRIS <ref>" returns (Excelsior Springs 2012 to 2018, the 2018 substation, Lee's Summit districts); their inventories list address, name, date and description of every building, so grep for street names. The NARA S3 copies answer 403.
- Where no form reads, chamber-of-commerce, city, Missouri Preservation, Clio and Waymarking pages often quote the form's wording, and web search summaries of the NPS record gave architects and former names. A place with no Wikipedia article, Clio entry or news story could not be written up.
- **Kansas City lists:** the Register rows are in "National Register of Historic Places listings in Jackson County, Missouri: Downtown Kansas City" and "...: Kansas City other" (191 rows); the plain Kansas City titles have no rows or do not exist. The rest of Jackson County is 52 rows, mostly small Lee's Summit house districts; Cass County adds 7. Many "Kansas City other" entries are plain apartment blocks, a poor guide to what is worth a visit. Wikipedia's "Historic Houses of Kansas City, Missouri" navbox (`wiki_raw.py "Template:..."`, then read research/wikiraw) lists houses by neighbourhood.
- **Worked out:** the Register lists for Lafayette, Cass, Ray, Jackson, Johnson, Clay and Platte (and on the Kansas side Leavenworth, Miami, Douglas, Wyandotte) are used up, and the Lee's Summit, Raytown, Grandview and Independence ground was found worked out by four sessions; do not re-compare them. The downtown Kansas City lists still held many names the Atlas lacked (compare against `tools/data_*.py`). Better ground: the Kansas City local register, Wikipedia building categories for the city, county historical society tours, and the 99 vanished places and old quarries round Lexington (Lexington Quarry, Macedonia Squarry). `choose.py --claim ... --state mo` reports a region with only 17 places, but the Kansas City ground is `kcmo`.
- **Clio** tour pages are a good source for Kansas City: `page.py --all -n 60000 https://theclio.com/tour/<id>` prints every stop's text (without `-n` it stops at 12,000 characters); each stop's page `/tour/<id>/<n>` adds more. Tours used: 2305 (Crossroads), 2310 (Financial, Library and Garment districts), 251 (downtown), 573 (Power and Light), 3025 (West Bottoms, stops 1 to 31: year, builder, later tenants, current use), 3153 (`-n 80000`). Tour pages carry no addresses.
- Other Kansas City sources: the African American Heritage Trail (aahtkc.org, sitemap at `/sitemap.xml`; 18th and Vine and the east side, what survives or was demolished); Flatland KC; the Kansas City Auto Museum; wornallmajors.org; the Banneker School Foundation (`bannekerschoolparkvillemo.org/history`); ccaal-garrisonschool.org (its history page 403, the home page opens).
- **Commons:** the photographer Mwkruse photographed most Kansas City Register listings (and one photographer most of its Register houses in 2015), files named for the building: `photo_find.py` found pictures for 13 of 14 houses and 7 of 18 West Bottoms places, and they settle red brick. Midtown and Country Club houses are worth a pictures pass.
- **Hours:** Missouri State Parks hours pages are at `https://mostateparks.com/hours/<number>/historic-site/<slug>`; the number is only in the site page's links (grep the cached page in `research/pages/` for `href="/hours/`), guessed `/park/<slug>/hours` addresses come back empty, and the park's main page may answer 404. VisitMO's museum pages give hours. The Ray County Museum's visit page (`raycountymuseum.org/home/visit/`) carries hours and a county driving tour; `jocomohistory.org/national-register-sites-in-johnson-county/` says which places are closed, gone or by appointment; `historiclexington.com` `?p=` pages lead to each place.
- **Pins:** Register points for Kansas City houses can be 200 to 300 m off (Knight, Tension, Katz) and Wikipedia's were 100 to 200 m off in 4 of 14 Johnson County cases; `geo.py` with the street address landed on the building every time, and beat the list point for 12 of 15 Kansas City buildings. But two buildings on a block can geocode to one point, and in the West Bottoms the Esri point is on the right block but sometimes the wrong building (a gas station). Downtown, Wikipedia's article points are often a block off and the Register list points were better (Chambers, Graphic Arts, Argyle, Rieger). `nom.py "<number> <street>, Kansas City, MO"` returns OpenStreetMap buildings named for the house (Keith, 5011 Sunset). The Wikipedia point for Excelsior Springs' Commercial West district is about 1 km from downtown. `parking.py` lists street names, not lots, in dense Kansas City.
- NPS "places" pages exist for Santa Fe Trail houses and read fine. HMdb marker pages answer 403; web search results quote them well enough for a date and a style.
- Refusing `page.py`: kcmo.gov, the Episcopal cathedral's and the Masonic Temple's sites (a web search quoted their hours), pendergastkc.org, rosinpreservation.com, maramecspringpark.com and marktwainmuseum.org (403), westonhistoricalmuseum.org and holytrinitycatholic.org (403), pchs1882.org (resets), `esmuseum.com/plan-your-visit` (almost no text).

### Montana
- Historic Montana (`historicmt.org/items/show/<n>`) and the NPGallery text forms of the 1970s to 2000s read well; a 2020 listing (Glenn's Dam) came back with only 893 characters. discoveranaconda.com answers 403.
- The Register list point for the Old Montana Prison is on a house across Main Street from the prison wall.
- Montana Wikipedia points of standing buildings were often 75 to 400 m off (8 of 21); `nom.py "<name>, <town>"` found most, and `osmnear.py ... buildings -g 'church|mission'` found St. Mary's Mission.
- `fwp.mt.gov/stateparks/<name>` pages put the hours in one line ("Seasons & Hours ... Open all year"): use `--all` and grep.
- Historic Montana answers `?output=mobile-json` on any item (`/items/show/<n>?output=mobile-json`) and on a search (`/items/browse?search=<words>&output=mobile-json`): the sign text, address and latitude and longitude for every Register sign, quicker than the HTML (item 9's HTML answered 500 while its JSON worked).
- helenahistory.org has a page for almost every lost Helena building with demolition years; its school, hospital and hotel index pages list them.
- miningmuseum.org answers 403; fortpecktheatre.org and fortbenton.com (Grand Union Hotel) are drawn by script; `saintmarysmission.org/visit` is 404.

### Nebraska
- history.nebraska.gov and outdoornebraska.gov (every Game and Parks page) answer 403 to `page.py`, but the web fetch tool read history.nebraska.gov's site pages unattended, and reads outdoornebraska.gov. NPS `nps.gov/places/<name>.htm` pages exist for state parks on the trails (Rock Creek Station, Fort Kearny, with an entrance coordinate) and for many downtown Omaha buildings on the "Travel Omaha" itinerary (address, current owner; guesses for the First National Bank, Central High, Hill Hotel and Blackstone were 404).
- Brownville's 1970 district form (`npgallery.nps.gov/NRHP/GetAsset/NRHP/70000374_text`) describes 24 buildings with dates and builders. The Carnegie multiple-property document (NPGallery 64500387) has a Section G table of every Nebraska Carnegie library with architect, style and whether it stands.
- The Nebraska City Museum Association page (`NebraskaCityMuseums.org/museums`) lists every museum, but the hours blocks cannot be matched to one museum with certainty: use each museum's own site (Wildwood's and Kregel's read). The ArcGIS geocoder gave PointAddresses for most Nebraska City and Brownville addresses; Wildwood's came back interpolated and off the house.
- Omaha: KETV's "Forgotten Omaha" pieces say which old hotels were torn down (the Hotel Castle). omahamagazine.com is drawn by script; Omaha Public Library's location pages answered 404.
- Refusing `page.py`: visitnebraska.com, nebraskapassport.com (403), lewisdredge.org (no connection).

### Nevada
- parks.nv.gov pages put hours and fees in the "Park Detail" block (`--all`, grep "Hours"). travelnevada.com pages often give hours and fees where the runner's site fails; several old addresses (`/ghost-town/belmont/`) are 404, so use the one a web search returns. shpo.nv.gov marker pages (`/nevadas-historical-markers/historical-markers/<slug>`) give each state marker's text. washoecounty.gov's Bowers Mansion page carries next year's tour dates in one block after "Tour Information". `ednv.org/find-a-church` lists every Episcopal church with its service time.
- ArcGIS address lookups with a house number landed on the building in Reno, Hawthorne, Mesquite and Las Vegas where Register points fell in a road or on a house. The pin check has no 1 m LiDAR for several rural pins (Austin, Eureka, Ward, Pioche, Delamar).
- Refusing `page.py`: nnry.com (403; elyrailroadmuseum.org gives the depot museum's hours), eurekacountynv.gov, cityofnorthlasvegas.com, piochenevada.com, recordcourier.com (403), wpmuseum.org (404), thesutrotunnel.org (resets).

### New Brunswick
- The Register's address line (the text between "Share | -->" and "Formally Recognized" in a cached page) fed to the ArcGIS geocoder sat on the building for 13 of 15 addresses, a better start than the Wikipedia county lists (a few rows hold a comment instead of a number); it missed the Edmundston cathedral (across the street), Greenock Church and Hammond House, where OpenStreetMap's named building was right.
- mcadamstation.ca and loyalisthouse.com answered 403.

### New Hampshire
- State park addresses are now `nhstateparks.org/find-parks-trails/<name>` (`mt-washington-state-park`); the older `/visit/state-parks/...` form answers 404 except for Weeks State Park.
- The White Mountain National Forest road status page (`fs.usda.gov/r09/whitemountain/alerts/forest-roads-status`) lists every forest road open or closed with a date (Sawyer River Road, the way to Livermore, is closed to vehicles).
- `whitemountainhistory.org` reads in full with `page.py --all` and has a page on each abandoned logging town. The Nature Conservancy's preserve pages read in full too, with the trail map PDF and a directions coordinate in the cached HTML.
- The Army Corps New England District pages (`nae.usace.army.mil`) answer 403; the Corps Lakes Gateway page (`corpslakes.erdc.dren.mil/visitors/projects.cfm?ID=...`) reads and lists a project's recreation areas. The Historical Society of Cheshire County's "Monadnock Moments" pages answer 404.
- NH State Parks' Cheshire Rail Trail page gives a Troy parking coordinate (42.724676, -72.184249) that is in Massachusetts: do not copy it.
- The state outline puts the whole Connecticut River in Vermont at Bedell Bridge and the Mount Orne bridge: pin a New Hampshire river place on the New Hampshire bank (`check.py` now warns within a kilometre instead of refusing).
- Register list points in Grafton County fell in rivers twice (Piermont, Blair) and in a yard once (Orford).
- The Wentworth-Coolidge page is `/find-parks-trails/wentworth-coolidge-mansion`, not `...-historic-site` (404).
- Refusing: friendsofpulpitrocktower.org now redirects to a Facebook login; weekshouse.org, newingtonnh.org and raymondnhhistoricalsociety.org would not connect.

### New Jersey
- Journey Through Jersey (`journeythroughjersey.com/sites/<name>`) reads well and gives hours for state parks and historic sites whose own `nj.gov/dep` pages give 7 characters.
- nps.gov reads well: `nps.gov/morr/planyourvisit/hours.htm` gives every Morristown site's hours (`nps.gov/places/wick-house.htm` and `ford-mansion.htm` are 404). morrisparks.net and morriscountynj.gov answer 403.
- `fws.gov/refuge/<name>/visit-us` answered 404; the refuge's main page (`fws.gov/refuge/supawna-meadows`) has hours and rules.
- On the Delaware shore by Fort Mott and Finn's Point the New Jersey and Delaware outlines overlap; `check.py` used to refuse such pins as Delaware and now accepts a pin inside either.
- Journey Through Jersey slugs that worked: `batsto`, `atsion-mansion`, `burlington-county-prison-museum`, `historic-smithville-park`, `north-pemberton-railroad-station-museum` (`kirbys-mill`, `peachfield`, `whitesbog-village`, `whitesbog` are 404). The hours sit in the first 700 characters of `page.py --all`.
- The Pinelands Alliance ghost-town page (`page.py --all -n 7000`) gives one line on what is left of Atsion, Batsto, Friendship, Hampton Furnace, Harrisville, Hermann, Martha, Pasadena and Weymouth.
- Newark has no Register list of its own; the Essex County list holds every Newark row. The ArcGIS point for 465 Dr. Martin Luther King Jr. Boulevard (Hall of Records) is 140 m west and 1035 Broad Street (South Park Presbyterian) on Lincoln Park's lawn; `nom.py` and `osmnear.py ... buildings -g` had both by name.
- Carnegie libraries: Dubicki's article (New Jersey Studies 3, 2017, free PDF on njs.libraries.rutgers.edu) tables every grant with dates, amounts, openings and whether each is still a library; Wikipedia's list (its "razed" marks come from a 1996 walking-club survey) is wrong in places: Union Hill was torn down in 2026, Orange's 348 Main Street is the Stickler library, and Lakewood may stand.

### New Mexico
- nmhistoricsites.org gives `page.py` the same generic text for every address; Fort Stanton's hours came from fortstanton.org/information.
- The Old Dowlin Mill's Register point is on Sudderth Drive about 280 m east of the mill; OpenStreetMap's "Old Mill" and the geocoder's point for 641 Sudderth agree.
- Fort Selden's page (`nmhistoricsites.org/fort-selden`) also gave only the CulturePass line; its hours stay unread.
- The federal land line labels the Kit Carson House in central Taos "Taos Pueblo".
- Refusing `page.py`: taos.org, kitcarsonmuseum.org, sanfelipedeneri.org, blueswallowmotel.com (403 or no connection); holychimayo.us/schedule is drawn by script and empty.

### New York
- Mohawk Valley Museums (`mohawkvalleymuseums.us/museums/<name>/`) has address, phone and season hours for most churches, museums and societies in Herkimer and Montgomery counties.
- The American Canal Society's sheets give a coordinate for every lock and aqueduct: the Enlarged Erie sheet's condition column drifts out of line from Lock 40 west (read eriecanal.org for those), and the Champlain sheet writes degrees with "E" for the degree sign (`42E46'48.92"N`); locks 1 to 9 matched OpenStreetMap ruin leads within about 10 m.
- Schenectady County put up markers along the Empire State Trail in 2024–2025 (Lock 7, Lock 8, the Niskayuna station, the Kiwanis Park aqueduct, HMdb 272279), each with a history and an exact position.
- Local Waterfront Revitalization Program PDFs (`docs.dos.ny.gov/opd-lwrp/...`) can hold a building-by-building historic inventory (Cape Vincent's tied the Duvillard Mill to the DEC Fisheries Station).
- Orleans Hub (`orleanshub.com/?p=<n>`) reads in full with `page.py --all` and its local-history series is the best source for Orleans County (cut the menu at "legal/meeting notices"); orleanscountytourism.com reads with the www address only.
- The Spindle City Historic Society's tour PDFs (`spindlecity.net/Brochures/...`) give dates and present uses building by building for Cohoes, and name which Erie lock walls can be seen.
- The Historic House Trust (`historichousetrust.org/houses/<name>/`) gives owner, operator and hours for every city-owned house in New York City; NYC Parks addresses under `php1-84.nycgovparks.org` answer 401, the same path on `www.nycgovparks.org` reads. Fort Wadsworth's hours are at `nps.gov/gate/learn/historyculture/fort-wadsworth.htm`.
- 44lakes.com (Fulton County tourism) has plain pages for both Johnstown-area museums and every county marker; johnstownnyhistsoc.org and fultoncountyhistoricalsociety.org return nothing usable.
- The Guilderland Path Through History flyer (guilderlandschools.org) lists which town houses open and when. albanyhilltowns.com would not connect to `page.py` but the web fetch tool read it. altamontenterprise.com shows a paywall to some articles.
- `schenectadyhistorical.org/site/mabee-farm-historic-site/` has the Mabee Farm's hours (the old schenectadyhistory.org address is 404); the Duanesburg parish is `christchurchduanesburg.org`.
- Refusing `page.py`: dcha-ny.org, townofausableny.gov, army.mil (an archived copy is used), duanesburg.gov, hoosickhistory.com's home page. `dec.ny.gov/places/valcour-island-primitive-area` is 404; `dec.ny.gov/places/lake-champlain-islands-complex` has Valcour's rules. boldtcastle.com's hours page still showed 2018 dates.
- The Valcour Island Heritage Trail guide (champlainvalleynhp.org) prints each waypoint just before its caption, not after.
- Points off the building: the Poke-O-Moonshine fire tower's Register point is at the road by the trailhead; the Kent-Delord House's Wikipedia point is 450 m west (`nom.py` gives 17 Cumberland Avenue); the Apple Tavern's is in a field 900 m east (the geocoder knows "Appel Inn"); the Fulton County courthouse's OpenStreetMap name sits on the county office building at 223 West Main Street.
- `parking.py` finds no lot at Montgomery Place's visitor centre (the lots it lists are Bard campus lots).
- **NPGallery forms in New York:** the `_text` route gives the one-page placeholder for nearly every older form, county after county (Albany, Greene, Sullivan, Delaware, Columbia, Madison, Oneida, Schoharie, Otsego, Chenango, Saratoga, St. Lawrence, Jefferson, Lewis, Chautauqua, Steuben, Montgomery, Warren, Rensselaer, the Colonie and Loudonville surveys), with text from about 2010 to 2017 (2011 on in Delaware County, 2014 on in Columbia and Schoharie; 14000009, 100000648, 100002367 and 100000650 read). For 2018 and later (100xxxxxx, some 2017) it returns an 893-byte PNG, which `page.py` now names as a picture (100003629 read).
